People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Well, I... I wouldn't say it's a significant decline, Doctor. I still get dressed every day and keep myself neat and tidy, I promise you that. I don't want you to think I'm just letting myself go to ruin.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want to say I've lost interest completely. I don't want you thinking I've just given up on things or become ungrateful. I still love my family just as much as ever, I promise you that. They mean the absolute world to me. 

But... well, I suppose I ought to be completely honest with you. There are a good many days lately where I just... I just sit at the kitchen table. I might sit there for hours and say very little. And I'll look out the window and know the garden needs tending, or I'll remember that choir practice is starting, but I just... I let them go by. I just don't feel like moving at all. 

I feel awful guilty about it, I truly do. It's not that I don't care, I just can't seem to find the get-up-and-go. Do you think that's just a normal part of being an old man, Doctor? Just needing to sit quietly for a spell? I sure hope my wife isn't too disappointed in me for being so dull these days.

Well, Doctor... I want to be as helpful as I can, so I'll try to explain it right. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hearing things every single second of the day, because I'm not. I still have plenty of quiet moments, I promise.

Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want you to think it's all tangled up in something bad or frightening. I try to keep a good head on my shoulders about these things, I really do. 

To tell you the truth, it's quite separate from those messages. It's just... well, some afternoons, when the house gets awful quiet, I'll hear him. I'll just hear my old friend call my name right from the front porch. Or sometimes he's just humming a nice old choir song we used to know. 

It's clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there with me. But it's a comforting sound, really, not a warning or anything upsetting. I hope it's alright that I hear him that way. Well, Doctor... I suppose this is the part where you might think I'm getting a bit dotty in my old age. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for saying this, because I know exactly how it must sound to an educated man like yourself. 

No, he... he isn't with us anymore. He passed away. It's been about a year and five months now since we lost him. And... well, to be perfectly honest with you, that's right around when all this started happening. Just after he passed.

I don't want you thinking I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just that hearing him now and then... well, it brings me a little comfort. That's not too terrible a thing, is it, Doctor? I just hope I'm still making sense to you.

additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and manic_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During my bright, restless weeks when I am sleeping little and making big plans, I still hear my friend’s voice and feel the radio or domino men are sending me warnings.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: manic_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness\n  manifestation: Since all this started, more months have been taken up by those high-energy spells and their aftereffects than by my calmer stretches.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode\n  manifestation: There was a quiet month when I was sleeping normally and not full of big plans, but I still heard my friend on the porch and ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor... I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I still get up every day, get dressed, make my meals... I try to keep things as normal as possible, I really do. 

But... I suppose if I'm being completely honest, things have slipped a little bit. Not terribly, mind you! Just... well, I haven't been making it to choir rehearsals like I ought to. And my garden, which I usually take such pride in, has gotten a bit overgrown in spots. I just haven't had the energy for it. 

And the domino games with the fellas... well, I think I made things a little uncomfortable a few times when I was worried about the messages, so I just... I haven't been joining them lately. It's better that way, so I don't bother anyone. 

It's not a complete disaster, Doctor, I promise. Just a few things here and there. That's fairly normal for someone my age going through a rough patch, isn't it? I hope you don't think I've just given up entirely.

Well, Doctor... it's not all the time, I promise. It's really just... well, maybe some afternoons. Usually when my wife is out running errands and the house gets awful quiet.

But it's just him, and it's just those quiet afternoons. It's not a constant bother or anything that keeps me from going about my day. I hope that doesn't sound too strange to you... it's just a little bit of comfort, really. That's alright, isn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor... I know I probably ought to say it's just my imagination playing tricks on me. I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I try so hard to keep my head on straight. 

But... if I'm being completely truthful with you, because I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer... it doesn't feel like just a memory. On those quiet afternoons, it sounds like an actual voice coming from right outside. It's so clear, Doctor, just exactly like he is standing right there on the porch calling my name, or humming that choir song. I'll even turn my head to look for him sometimes. 

I know it sounds a bit foolish for an old man to say, but it really does sound like it's coming from outside of me, in the real world. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for admitting that... is it okay that it happens like that? I just want to be honest with you.

Is it... is it helpful for you to know that it's been going on that long, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your old friend’s voice, is it connected to the messages or warnings you believe people are giving you, or does it happen independently of those beliefs?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want you to think it's all tangled up in something bad or frightening. I try to keep a good head on my shoulders about these things, I really do. \n\nTo tell you the truth, it's quite separate from those messages. It's just... well, some afternoons, when the house gets awful quiet, I'll hear him. I'll just hear my old friend call my name right from the front porch. Or sometimes he's just humming a nice old choir song we used to know. \n\nIt's clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there with me. But it's a comforting sound, really, not a warning or anything upsetting. I hope it's alright that I hear him that way. It doesn't mean my mind is completely gone, does it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how it happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your old friend still alive?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I suppose this is the part where you might think I'm getting a bit dotty in my old age. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for saying this, because I know exactly how it must sound to an educated man like yourself. \n\nNo, he... he isn't with us anymore. He passed away. It's been about a year and five months now since we lost him. And... well, to be perfectly honest with you, that's right around when all this started happening. Just after he passed. \n\nI know it probably sounds foolish, an old man hearing the voice of someone who's already gone to be with the Lord. But I promise you, I know he's gone, I truly do. I don't want you thinking I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just that hearing him now and then... well, it brings me a little comfort. That's not too terrible a thing, is it, Doctor? I just hope I'm still making sense to you.\n\nDoctor: Compared with before these experiences began, has there been a clear and substantial decline in how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I still get up every day, get dressed, make my meals... I try to keep things as normal as possible, I really do. \n\nBut... I suppose if I'm being completely honest, things have slipped a little bit. Not terribly, mind you! Just... well, I haven't been making it to choir rehearsals like I ought to. And my garden, which I usually take such pride in, has gotten a bit overgrown in spots. I just haven't had the energy for it. \n\nAnd the domino games with the fellas... well, I think I made things a little uncomfortable a few times when I was worried about the messages, so I just... I haven't been joining them lately. It's better that way, so I don't bother anyone. \n\nIt's not a complete disaster, Doctor, I promise. Just a few things here and there. That's fairly normal for someone my age going through a rough patch, isn't it? My wife, bless her heart, she always tells me it's just regular talk and I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. I try to keep a good head on my shoulders about these things, I really do. \n\nTo tell you the truth, it's quite separate from those messages. It's just... well, some afternoons, when the house gets awful quiet, I'll hear him. I'll just hear my old friend call my name right from the front porch. Or sometimes he's just humming a nice old choir song we used to know. \n\nIt's clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there with me. But it's a comforting sound, really, not a warning or anything upsetting. I hope it's alright that I hear him that way. It doesn't mean my mind is completely gone, does it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how it happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your old friend still alive?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I suppose this is the part where you might think I'm getting a bit dotty in my old age. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for saying this, because I know exactly how it must sound to an educated man like yourself. \n\nNo, he... he isn't with us anymore. He passed away. It's been about a year and five months now since we lost him. And... well, to be perfectly honest with you, that's right around when all this started happening. Just after he passed. \n\nI know it probably sounds foolish, an old man hearing the voice of someone who's already gone to be with the Lord. But I promise you, I know he's gone, I truly do. I don't want you thinking I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just that hearing him now and then... well, it brings me a little comfort. That's not too terrible a thing, is it, Doctor? I just hope I'm still making sense to you.\n\nDoctor: Compared with before these experiences began, has there been a clear and substantial decline in how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I still get up every day, get dressed, make my meals... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor... it's really not very much time at all. I promise it doesn't take up my whole day or keep me from doing my chores around the house. 

Like I said before, it's mostly just... well, some afternoons. When my wife is out and the house gets so awfully quiet. I'll just be sitting there, and for a minute or two, I'll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of those old choir songs. It sounds so clear, just like he's standing right there. But it's only for a little bit, just during those quiet afternoons. 

I hope you don't think I'm sitting around listening to voices all day long. I try to stay busy, I really do. It's just a little bit of time here and there... that's not too bad, is it, Doctor?

I just haven't had the energy for it. \n\nAnd the domino games with the fellas... well, I think I made things a little uncomfortable a few times when I was worried about the messages, so I just... Well, Doctor... I hope I've been making good sense to you today. I try my very best to keep my words straight, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm getting senile or losing my wits.

Just every now and then, my thoughts will start running from the church choir, and then straight over to baseball, and then to some repairs I need to do around the house, and then to my friend... and then right back again.

I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for saying this, because I know exactly how it must sound to an educated man like yourself. \n\nNo, he... he isn't with us anymore. He passed away. It's been about a year and five months now since we lost him. And... well, to be perfectly honest with you, that's right around when all this started happening. I just hope I'm still making sense to you.\n\nDoctor: Compared with before these experiences began, has there been a clear and substantial decline in how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I still get up every day, get dressed, make my meals... I try to keep things as normal as possible, I really do. \n\nBut... I suppose if I'm being completely honest, things have slipped a little bit. Not terribly, mind you! Just... well, I haven't been making it to choir rehearsals like I ought to. And my garden, which I usually take such pride in, has gotten a bit overgrown in spots. I just haven't had the energy for it. \n\nAnd the domino games with the fellas... well, I think I made things a little uncomfortable a few times when I was worried about the messages, so I just... I haven't been joining them lately. It's better that way, so I don't bother anyone. \n\nIt's not a complete disaster, Doctor, I promise. Just a few things here and there. That's fairly normal for someone my age going through a rough patch, isn't it? I hope you don't think I've just given up entirely.\n\nDoctor: Besides believing that the men at the domino table and the radio announcer are sending messages to you, have you had any other beliefs that your wife or other people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... But... well, I just know in my gut they're warning me to be careful. \n\nBut that's the only thing, I promise you. I don't go around believing a whole bunch of other strange things. I hope that's alright? It sounds so clear, just like he's standing right there. But it's only for a little bit, just during those quiet afternoons. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm sitting around listening to voices all day long. I try to stay busy, I really do. It's just a little bit of time here and there... that's not too bad, is it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts ever felt confused or jumbled, making it difficult for you to organize or express what you mean?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want to confuse anybody or talk their ear off with nonsense. \n\nI mean... sometimes I might lose my train of thought for just a second, but who doesn't at my age, right? I always make sure to speak politely and clear enough for my wife and the neighbors to understand me. I don't think anyone has ever complained about not being able to follow what I'm saying. \n\nThat's a good thing, isn't it, Doctor? That my mind is still sharp enough to hold a proper conversation? I sure hope so.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want to say I've lost interest completely. I don't want you thinking I've just given up on things or become ungrateful. I still love my family just as much as ever, I promise you that. They mean the absolute world to me. \n\nBut... well, I suppose I ought to be completely honest with you. There are a good many days lately where I just... I just sit at the kitchen table. I might sit there for hours and say very little. And I'll look out the window and know the garden needs tending, or I'll remember that choir practice is starting, but I just... I let them go by. I just don't feel like moving at all. \n\nI feel awful guilty about it, I truly do. It's not that I don't care, I just can't seem to find the get-up-and-go. Do you think that's just a normal part of being an old man, Doctor? Just needing to sit quietly for a spell? I sure hope my wife isn't too disappointed in me for being so dull these days.\n\nDoctor: During these nine months, was there a period of at least one month when you regularly experienced either the messages or your old friend’s voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I want to be as helpful as I can, so I'll try to explain it right. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hearing things every single second of the day, because I'm not. I still have plenty of quiet moments, I promise. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose if I look back over these past nine months, they've been there on and off. The messages from the TV and the radio, and... well, my old friend's voice. Sometimes it might be quiet for a little bit, but they always seem to come back around. I guess it has been a fairly regular thing for most of this time. \n\nI really try not to let it bother anyone else, though. I keep it to myself as best I can. Is it... is it helpful for you to know that it's been going on that long, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your old friend’s voice, is it connected to the messages or warnings you believe people are giving you, or does it happen independently of those beliefs?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want you to think it's all tangled up in something bad or frightening. I try to keep a good head on my shoulders about these things, I really do. \n\nTo tell you the truth, it's quite separate from those messages. It's just... well, some afternoons, when the house gets awful quiet, I'll hear him. I'll just hear my old friend call my name right from the front porch. Or sometimes he's just humming a nice old choir song we used to know. \n\nIt's clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there with me. But it's a comforting sound, really, not a warning or anything upsetting. I hope it's alright that I hear him that way. It doesn't mean my mind is completely gone, does it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how it happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your old friend still alive?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I suppose this is the part where you might think I'm getting a bit dotty in my old age. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for saying this, because I know exactly how it must sound to an educated man like yourself. \n\nNo, he... he isn't with us anymore. He passed away. It's been about a year and five months now since we lost him. And... well, to be perfectly honest with you, that's right around when all this started happening. Just after he passed. \n\nI know it probably sounds foolish, an old man hearing the voice of someone who's already gone to be with the Lord. But I promise you, I know he's gone, I truly do. I don't want you thinking I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just that hearing him now and then... well, it brings me a little comfort. That's not too terrible a thing, is it, Doctor? I just hope I'm still making sense to you.\n\nDoctor: Compared with before these experiences began, has there been a clear and substantial decline in how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I still get up every day, get dressed, make my meals... I try to keep things as normal as possible, I really do. \n\nBut... I suppose if I'm being completely honest, things have slipped a little bit. Not terribly, mind you! Just... well, I haven't been making it to choir rehearsals like I ought to. And my garden, which I usually take such pride in, has gotten a bit overgrown in spots. I just haven't had the energy for it. \n\nAnd the domino games with the fellas... well, I think I made things a little uncomfortable a few times when I was worried about the messages, so I just... I haven't been joining them lately. It's better that way, so I don't bother anyone. \n\nIt's not a complete disaster, Doctor, I promise. Just a few things here and there. That's fairly normal for someone my age going through a rough patch, isn't it? I hope you don't think I've just given up entirely.\n\nDoctor: Besides believing that the men at the domino table and the radio announcer are sending messages to you, have you had any other beliefs that your wife or other people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I hope you don't think my mind is just running completely wild with all sorts of crazy ideas. I really try to keep myself grounded, I do. \n\nTo be honest with you, no sir, there's nothing else. It's really just... just what we were talking about. The men at the domino table, and sometimes that announcer on the radio. Like I was saying, they give those little messages meant just for me. My wife, bless her heart, she always tells me it's just regular talk and I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. But... well, I just know in my gut they're warning me to be careful. \n\nBut that's the only thing, I promise you. I don't go around believing a whole bunch of other strange things. I hope that's alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making myself sound worse than I am.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear your old friend’s voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... it's not all the time, I promise. It's really just... well, maybe some afternoons. Usually when my wife is out running errands and the house gets awful quiet. \n\nSometimes, I'll just be sitting there, and... I'll hear him call my name from out on the porch. Or maybe I'll hear him humming one of the old choir songs we used to sing together. It sounds so clear, Doctor, just like he's standing right there in the room with me. \n\nBut it's just him, and it's just those quiet afternoons. It's not a constant bother or anything that keeps me from going about my day. I hope that doesn't sound too strange to you... it's just a little bit of comfort, really. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you hear your friend’s voice, do you believe it is an actual voice coming from outside you, or does it feel more like remembering or imagining his voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I know I probably ought to say it's just my imagination playing tricks on me. I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I try so hard to keep my head on straight. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely truthful with you, because I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer... it doesn't feel like just a memory. On those quiet afternoons, it sounds like an actual voice coming from right outside. It's so clear, Doctor, just exactly like he is standing right there on the porch calling my name, or humming that choir song. I'll even turn my head to look for him sometimes. \n\nI know it sounds a bit foolish for an old man to say, but it really does sound like it's coming from outside of me, in the real world. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for admitting that... is it okay that it happens like that? I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that the men at the domino table and the radio announcer are sending those messages specifically to you?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I mean, I know my wife thinks I'm just being silly about it. She says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, and I suppose maybe she's right to worry about how it sounds. I don't want you to think I'm just making things up to be difficult.\n\nBut... well, I am certain. When I'm sitting there, or listening to the radio, it's just so clear to me. Even when she says it's just regular talk, I know in my heart they are warning me to be careful. It's just a feeling I get, a knowing, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too stubborn... I just can't shake the feeling that those little messages are meant just for me. That's not too terrible to think, is it?\n\nDoctor: About how much of your time do you spend hearing your friend’s voice or other voices?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... it's really not very much time at all. When my wife is out and the house gets so awfully quiet. I'll just be sitting there, and for a minute or two, I'll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of those old choir songs. It sounds so clear, just like he's standing right there. It's just a little bit of time here and there... that's not too bad, is it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts ever felt confused or jumbled, making it difficult for you to organize or express what you mean?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor... I know he's passed on. I really do. I went to the funeral and everything, so I know he's not... well, not truly there in the flesh. I don't want you to think I've completely lost touch with reality or anything like that.

It's just... some afternoons, when the house is so very quiet... I hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of our choir songs. And in that split second, it sounds so incredibly clear, just like he is standing right there beside me. For just a moment, it feels like he really is there. 

But I always remember right after that he's gone. I'm not crazy for feeling like he's there just for a second, am I? It's just because I miss him, I suppose. That makes sense, doesn't it, Doctor?

I try to be good and not make a fuss, you know. \n\nBut... well, I guess I came in because... My wife, she says it is just regular talk, but... I know they are warning me to be careful. \n\nI hope it's alright that I'm telling you this. Am I doing okay explaining it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you heard voices that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Oh, well... I sure hope you don't think less of me for saying this. I try to be real sensible, I do. But... yes, sometimes. Not all the time, mind you! Just... some afternoons, when the house gets real quiet. \n\nI, uh... I hear my old friend. He'll call my name from out on the porch, or sometimes I can hear him humming an old choir song. It's clear as day, Doctor, just exactly like he is standing right there. It's a nice sound, really, nothing bad or scary, I promise. I don't make a fuss over it. \n\nI hope that's an okay thing to tell you. I'm not sounding too crazy for hearing it, am I?\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these messages and hearing your old friend’s voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I suppose it's been... oh, maybe around nine months now? Give or take a little. But I promise you, it's really just on and off. It's not an everyday thing, it surely isn't. I wouldn't want you to think I'm just sitting around listening to it all day long. I try to be good and just go about my business so it doesn't get in anyone's way. \n\nI hope I didn't wait too long to mention it... I just didn't want to be a nuisance to my wife or to the doctors, you know? Does nine months seem like an awful long time to you? I sure hope I'm doing the right thing by telling you now.\n\nDoctor: Since these experiences began, have you had a significant decline in your ability to work, manage daily responsibilities, or care for yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I wouldn't say it's a significant decline, Doctor. I still get dressed every day and keep myself neat and tidy, I promise you that. I don't want you to think I'm just letting myself go to ruin. \n\nBut... well, I suppose I have missed a few choir rehearsals lately. Just a few! And... oh, I hate to admit it, but parts of my garden have gotten a little overgrown. I just haven't had the energy to keep up with the weeding. And... well, the domino games... I guess I made things a little uncomfortable down there with the fellas, what with the messages and all. I just... I haven't been joining them like I used to. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too lazy or difficult, Doctor. I really do try my best to keep up with things. Is it... is it normal to fall behind a little bit like that?\n\nDoctor: Have other people told you that your speech or conversation has become difficult to follow?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no. No, I wouldn't say that at all. I try to be real careful about how I speak to folks, I really do. I wouldn't want to confuse anybody or talk their ear off with nonsense. \n\nI mean... sometimes I might lose my train of thought for just a second, but who doesn't at my age, right? I always make sure to speak politely and clear enough for my wife and the neighbors to understand me. I don't think anyone has ever complained about not being able to follow what I'm saying. \n\nThat's a good thing, isn't it, Doctor? That my mind is still sharp enough to hold a proper conversation? I sure hope so.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want to say I've lost interest completely. I don't want you thinking I've just given up on things or become ungrateful. I still love my family just as much as ever, I promise you that. They mean the absolute world to me. \n\nBut... well, I suppose I ought to be completely honest with you. There are a good many days lately where I just... I just sit at the kitchen table. I might sit there for hours and say very little. And I'll look out the window and know the garden needs tending, or I'll remember that choir practice is starting, but I just... I let them go by. I just don't feel like moving at all. \n\nI feel awful guilty about it, I truly do. It's not that I don't care, I just can't seem to find the get-up-and-go. Do you think that's just a normal part of being an old man, Doctor? Just needing to sit quietly for a spell? I sure hope my wife isn't too disappointed in me for being so dull these days.\n\nDoctor: During these nine months, was there a period of at least one month when you regularly experienced either the messages or your old friend’s voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I want to be as helpful as I can, so I'll try to explain it right. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hearing things every single second of the day, because I'm not. I still have plenty of quiet moments, I promise. \n\nBut... yes, I suppose if I look back over these past nine months, they've been there on and off. The messages from the TV and the radio, and... well, my old friend's voice. Sometimes it might be quiet for a little bit, but they always seem to come back around. I guess it has been a fairly regular thing for most of this time. \n\nI really try not to let it bother anyone else, though. I keep it to myself as best I can. Is it... is it helpful for you to know that it's been going on that long, Doctor? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your old friend’s voice, is it connected to the messages or warnings you believe people are giving you, or does it happen independently of those beliefs?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I wouldn't want you to think it's all tangled up in something bad or frightening. I try to keep a good head on my shoulders about these things, I really do. \n\nTo tell you the truth, it's quite separate from those messages. It's just... well, some afternoons, when the house gets awful quiet, I'll hear him. I'll just hear my old friend call my name right from the front porch. Or sometimes he's just humming a nice old choir song we used to know. \n\nIt's clear as a bell, just exactly like he is standing right there with me. But it's a comforting sound, really, not a warning or anything upsetting. I hope it's alright that I hear him that way. It doesn't mean my mind is completely gone, does it, Doctor? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how it happens.\n\nDoctor: Is your old friend still alive?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I suppose this is the part where you might think I'm getting a bit dotty in my old age. I really hope you don't judge me too harshly for saying this, because I know exactly how it must sound to an educated man like yourself. \n\nNo, he... he isn't with us anymore. He passed away. It's been about a year and five months now since we lost him. And... well, to be perfectly honest with you, that's right around when all this started happening. Just after he passed. \n\nI know it probably sounds foolish, an old man hearing the voice of someone who's already gone to be with the Lord. But I promise you, I know he's gone, I truly do. I don't want you thinking I'm completely out of touch with reality. It's just that hearing him now and then... well, it brings me a little comfort. That's not too terrible a thing, is it, Doctor? I just hope I'm still making sense to you.\n\nDoctor: Compared with before these experiences began, has there been a clear and substantial decline in how you function overall?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely falling apart or anything. I still get up every day, get dressed, make my meals... I try to keep things as normal as possible, I really do. \n\nBut... I suppose if I'm being completely honest, things have slipped a little bit. Not terribly, mind you! Just... well, I haven't been making it to choir rehearsals like I ought to. And my garden, which I usually take such pride in, has gotten a bit overgrown in spots. I just haven't had the energy for it. \n\nAnd the domino games with the fellas... well, I think I made things a little uncomfortable a few times when I was worried about the messages, so I just... I haven't been joining them lately. It's better that way, so I don't bother anyone. \n\nIt's not a complete disaster, Doctor, I promise. Just a few things here and there. That's fairly normal for someone my age going through a rough patch, isn't it? I hope you don't think I've just given up entirely.\n\nDoctor: Besides believing that the men at the domino table and the radio announcer are sending messages to you, have you had any other beliefs that your wife or other people say are not true?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I hope you don't think my mind is just running completely wild with all sorts of crazy ideas. I really try to keep myself grounded, I do. \n\nTo be honest with you, no sir, there's nothing else. It's really just... just what we were talking about. The men at the domino table, and sometimes that announcer on the radio. Like I was saying, they give those little messages meant just for me. My wife, bless her heart, she always tells me it's just regular talk and I'm making a mountain out of a molehill. But... well, I just know in my gut they're warning me to be careful. \n\nBut that's the only thing, I promise you. I don't go around believing a whole bunch of other strange things. I hope that's alright? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers and not making myself sound worse than I am.\n\nDoctor: How often do you hear your old friend’s voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... it's not all the time, I promise. It's really just... well, maybe some afternoons. Usually when my wife is out running errands and the house gets awful quiet. \n\nSometimes, I'll just be sitting there, and... I'll hear him call my name from out on the porch. Or maybe I'll hear him humming one of the old choir songs we used to sing together. It sounds so clear, Doctor, just like he's standing right there in the room with me. \n\nBut it's just him, and it's just those quiet afternoons. It's not a constant bother or anything that keeps me from going about my day. I hope that doesn't sound too strange to you... it's just a little bit of comfort, really. That's alright, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: When you hear your friend’s voice, do you believe it is an actual voice coming from outside you, or does it feel more like remembering or imagining his voice?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I know I probably ought to say it's just my imagination playing tricks on me. I really don't want you to think I'm losing my mind or anything like that. I try so hard to keep my head on straight. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely truthful with you, because I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer... it doesn't feel like just a memory. On those quiet afternoons, it sounds like an actual voice coming from right outside. It's so clear, Doctor, just exactly like he is standing right there on the porch calling my name, or humming that choir song. I'll even turn my head to look for him sometimes. \n\nI know it sounds a bit foolish for an old man to say, but it really does sound like it's coming from outside of me, in the real world. I hope you don't think too poorly of me for admitting that... is it okay that it happens like that? I just want to be honest with you.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that the men at the domino table and the radio announcer are sending those messages specifically to you?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I mean, I know my wife thinks I'm just being silly about it. She says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, and I suppose maybe she's right to worry about how it sounds. I don't want you to think I'm just making things up to be difficult.\n\nBut... well, I am certain. When I'm sitting there, or listening to the radio, it's just so clear to me. Even when she says it's just regular talk, I know in my heart they are warning me to be careful. It's just a feeling I get, a knowing, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too stubborn... I just can't shake the feeling that those little messages are meant just for me. That's not too terrible to think, is it?\n\nDoctor: About how much of your time do you spend hearing your friend’s voice or other voices?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... it's really not very much time at all. I promise it doesn't take up my whole day or keep me from doing my chores around the house. \n\nLike I said before, it's mostly just... well, some afternoons. When my wife is out and the house gets so awfully quiet. I'll just be sitting there, and for a minute or two, I'll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of those old choir songs. It sounds so clear, just like he's standing right there. But it's only for a little bit, just during those quiet afternoons. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm sitting around listening to voices all day long. I try to stay busy, I really do. It's just a little bit of time here and there... that's not too bad, is it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts ever felt confused or jumbled, making it difficult for you to organize or express what you mean?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I hope I've been making good sense to you today. I try my very best to keep my words straight, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm getting senile or losing my wits. \n\nBut... I suppose sometimes, if I'm being perfectly honest... my mind just gets to going a bit too fast. It's not all the time, I promise! Just every now and then, my thoughts will start running from the church choir, and then straight over to baseball, and then to some repairs I need to do around the house, and then to my friend... and then right back again. \n\nThey just race around so fast that... well, I can't seem to hold onto one single thought long enough to explain it right. It all gets a bit tangled up when I try to get the words out. I hope you don't mind me admitting that... it doesn't mean there's something terribly wrong with me, does it, Doctor? Am I explaining it okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear any voices other than your deceased friend’s?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, no... nothing like that. I promise it's just him. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hearing a whole crowd of strangers or anything frightening like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I mentioned, on some of those afternoons when the house gets so awfully quiet. That's the only time. I'll just hear my old friend call my name from out on the porch, or hear him humming one of our old choir songs. It's just him, clear as day, like he's standing right there. But nobody else, I assure you. \n\nThat's a lot better than hearing all sorts of different voices, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that puts your mind at ease about me. I'm doing my best to give you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your deceased friend’s voice, do you believe he is actually present, or do you recognize that it is an experience occurring even though you know he has died?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I know he's passed on. I really do. I went to the funeral and everything, so I know he's not... well, not truly there in the flesh. I don't want you to think I've completely lost touch with reality or anything like that.\n\nIt's just... some afternoons, when the house is so very quiet... I hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of our choir songs. And in that split second, it sounds so incredibly clear, just like he is standing right there beside me. For just a moment, it feels like he really is there. \n\nBut I always remember right after that he's gone. I'm not crazy for feeling like he's there just for a second, am I? It's just because I miss him, I suppose. That makes sense, doesn't it, Doctor?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I know my wife thinks I'm just being silly about it. She says I'm making a mountain out of a molehill, and I suppose maybe she's right to worry about how it sounds. I don't want you to think I'm just making things up to be difficult.\n\nBut... well, I am certain. When I'm sitting there, or listening to the radio, it's just so clear to me. Even when she says it's just regular talk, I know in my heart they are warning me to be careful. It's just a feeling I get, a knowing, you know? I hope that doesn't make me sound too stubborn... I just can't shake the feeling that those little messages are meant just for me. That's not too terrible to think, is it?\n\nDoctor: About how much of your time do you spend hearing your friend’s voice or other voices?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... it's really not very much time at all. I promise it doesn't take up my whole day or keep me from doing my chores around the house. \n\nLike I said before, it's mostly just... well, some afternoons. When my wife is out and the house gets so awfully quiet. I'll just be sitting there, and for a minute or two, I'll hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of those old choir songs. It sounds so clear, just like he's standing right there. But it's only for a little bit, just during those quiet afternoons. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm sitting around listening to voices all day long. I try to stay busy, I really do. It's just a little bit of time here and there... that's not too bad, is it, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have your thoughts ever felt confused or jumbled, making it difficult for you to organize or express what you mean?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I hope I've been making good sense to you today. I try my very best to keep my words straight, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm getting senile or losing my wits. \n\nBut... I suppose sometimes, if I'm being perfectly honest... my mind just gets to going a bit too fast. It's not all the time, I promise! Just every now and then, my thoughts will start running from the church choir, and then straight over to baseball, and then to some repairs I need to do around the house, and then to my friend... and then right back again. \n\nThey just race around so fast that... well, I can't seem to hold onto one single thought long enough to explain it right. It all gets a bit tangled up when I try to get the words out. I hope you don't mind me admitting that... it doesn't mean there's something terribly wrong with me, does it, Doctor? Am I explaining it okay for you?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear any voices other than your deceased friend’s?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, Doctor, no... nothing like that. I promise it's just him. I wouldn't want you to think I'm hearing a whole crowd of strangers or anything frightening like that. \n\nIt's really just... well, like I mentioned, on some of those afternoons when the house gets so awfully quiet. That's the only time. I'll just hear my old friend call my name from out on the porch, or hear him humming one of our old choir songs. It's just him, clear as day, like he's standing right there. But nobody else, I assure you. \n\nThat's a lot better than hearing all sorts of different voices, isn't it, Doctor? I hope that puts your mind at ease about me. I'm doing my best to give you the right answers.\n\nDoctor: When you hear your deceased friend’s voice, do you believe he is actually present, or do you recognize that it is an experience occurring even though you know he has died?\n\nPatient: Well, Doctor... I know he's passed on. I really do. I went to the funeral and everything, so I know he's not... well, not truly there in the flesh. I don't want you to think I've completely lost touch with reality or anything like that.\n\nIt's just... some afternoons, when the house is so very quiet... I hear him call my name from the porch, or hum one of our choir songs. And in that split second, it sounds so incredibly clear, just like he is standing right there beside me. For just a moment, it feels like he really is there. \n\nBut I always remember right after that he's gone. I'm not crazy for feeling like he's there just for a second, am I? It's just because I miss him, I suppose. That makes sense, doesn't it, Doctor?"
  }
